# Divergences: the C engine against the Java oracle

The C engine (csrc/engine, its playable client out/native/play) is checked
row by row against the Java oracle (vanilla 1.7.10 plus SPEC.md's pins and
config.yaml's switches). Sections 1 to 6 are that bridge, Java -> C. The
second bridge, C -> CUDA (a device kernel in csrc/cuda, or the result of
the pool in csrc/runtime, against the C engine), is section 7.

Generated by `csrc/tests/divergences.sh` from the ledger in
`csrc/tests/divergences/*.tsv` and the live sources named in each section;
never edit it by hand. A lane that finds or fixes a divergence adds its row to
`csrc/tests/divergences/LANE.tsv`; the merge regenerates this file.

As of commit 35ef765f (2026-10-01 6:47 pm MT).

## 1. Summary

Java -> C: 979 divergences in the ledger, 809 fixed, 94 open, 40 accepted, 36 superseded.
11 ledger lines have problems (listed at the end).

The sweep (master's out/sweep/causes.tsv) holds 25 clusters: 13 open, 0 assigned, 11 fixed.

The fuzzers have checked 17,242 sessions and 14,896,234 rows (the sweep 1,234 sessions, 2,334,761 rows in 5 rounds; before it, 16,008 sessions in the 42 of 48 fuzz rounds whose lane counted them). They found 117 new causes in those rounds, and the sweep 22 new clusters.

New causes per 1,000 sessions, the last rounds before the sweep (oldest first): chainfuzz 6.2, 9.3, 16.0, 6.1; playfuzz 0.0, 0.0; rawfuzz 102.8; rlfuzz 30.0, 15.0, 20.0, 10.0.
The sweep, its last rounds: 31.1, 12.1, 12.0, 24.4, 7.9.

## 2. Known and fixed (known knowns)

Every fixed divergence between the Java oracle and the C engine, grouped by area, newest
first, with the commit that fixed it on master and what pins it (a recording under out/java: snapshots,
clientframes or rawjudge; or a test).

### worldgen (14)

- **leavesfire-springdrop** (2026-09-27, lane/leavesfire): a population spring's immediate flow washed a dandelion away and native drew the drop's World.rand but spawned no EntityItem during population (the first snow scene at chunk 42,15, row 19); fixed by lane/portals (a population's washed-away drops reach the world in every context) and lane/seed1chain (f6b371bc). Fix `9568903d`, pin out/java/snapshots/portalmake-s1.
- **plant-sapling-s1** (2026-09-27, lane/physplants (audit A-08), field `d.sseed`): the sapling's new WorldGenBigTree(true) draws a Det.newRandom seeder long whatever the tree type (row 700), and a 2x2 spruce ran the mega pine's podzol pass, which only BiomeDecorator calls (an earlier recording row 2878 w.bc). Fix `36800e7f`, pin out/java/snapshots/plant-sapling-s1.
- **seed1chain-springflow** (2026-09-27, lane/seed1chain (GoldBot golden chain)): the overworld's population drops what a spring's immediate flow washes away; found on the old trek's S49 row 11659, not kept; lane/portals fixed the same cause (9568903d), pinned by portalmake-s1. Fix `f6b371bc`, pin out/java/snapshots/portalmake-s1.
- **struct-witchhut-s1** (2026-09-27, lane/structscenes (audit ST-01), field `w.ents`): SwampHut.addComponentParts spawns a witch when the hut is built; the replay dropped the record (sr_pop_ent took only villagers) and sc_witch_new skipped EntityLivingBase's three Math.random draws; master row 19 w.ents 32/31. Fix `3226415d`, pin out/java/snapshots/struct-witchhut-s1.
- **walk300-s1** (2026-09-27, lane/bigtree (lane/rerecaudit's re-records), field `w.bc`): native loaded the snapshot's WorldGenBigTree state (bigTree, jungle heightLimit) before seedworld_build rebuilt the spawn area, so the rebuild's jungle big trees grew from the end state instead of a fresh JVM's 0; 15 re-recorded seed-1 recordings failed (auditfix-far-s1 row 25 want 8 got 6, walk300-s1 row 62 d.ents). Fix `5ee45daa`, pin out/java/snapshots/walk300-s1.
- **anytick2-bigtree** (2026-09-26, lane/anytick2 (any-tick sweep), field `w.bc`): BiomeGenBase keeps one WorldGenBigTree per biome whose heightLimit is set once and never cleared, so the next big tree of that biome starts from it for the JVM's life; worldstate.nbt carries bigTree (gold-g14 t3466). Fix `ec4f328c`, pin make, -C, oracle, anytick, RECS=gold-g14-s42.
- **bot5-neighbourload** (2026-09-26, lane/bot5 (GoldBot golden chain)): Chunk.func_150809_p's getChunkFromBlockCoords for the east and south neighbours loads a chunk that is not loaded; a chicken reloaded from its chunk keeps the constructor's fresh egg timer (gold-g10). Fix `2bd73749`, pin out/java/snapshots/gold-g10-s42.
- **struct-cp-s42** (2026-09-26, lane/villages (work list B4)): a structure start read back from data/<Type>.dat keeps each component's box, HPos and VCount; native rebuilt it from the seed (the x 663 house at HPos 66 instead of 68, row 23). Fix `20f18972`, pin out/java/snapshots/struct-cp-s42.
- **bot3-fortress-order** (2026-09-25, lane/bot3): the fortress structure map did not keep Java's HashMap order (all three seed-42 fortress starts share one bucket, so the first sizeable start is Fortress.dat's first entry); Snapshot writes structures.json. Fix `a61e5a9a`, pin out/java/snapshots/gold-g7-s42.
- **populate-decorators** (2026-09-23, lane/populate (probe)): population divergences: mutated biomes run the base's decorator object with themselves as the biome (Savanna M, Flower Forest, Mega Spruce Taiga, roofed forest M, hills M differ again); WorldGenBlockBlob's radius draws are x, y, z; fallInstantly held for the whole populate call; a no-op setBlock attempt still runs the light pass. Fix `83da427`, pin make, -C, csrc, test, (test_populate).
- **populate-structmap-625** (2026-09-23, lane/popstruct (probe)): the structure maps also hold starts from initialWorldChunkLoad's 625 spawn chunks, and one extra entry over HashMap's 12 to 16 resize changes the whole map's iteration order (pop26 generated its starts in another order); the driver offers those chunks first. Fix `3e60a12b`, pin make, -C, csrc, test, (test_populate, pop26).
- **caves-topblock** (2026-09-22, lane/caves (chunk dumps)): both carvers put the biome object's current topBlock back over exposed dirt, and Hills, mega taiga and Savanna M rewrite that field per column (48 non-grass re-tops in the Extreme Hills regions); C operand order must be split where Java draws twice. Fix -, pin make, -C, csrc, test, (test_chunks).
- **worldgen-hills-seed** (2026-09-22, lane/worldgen (chunk dumps)): the Hills layer's second parent zoom chain is never world-seeded in vanilla; biome slot 161 holds the 160 object; Mesa spire noise comes from Random(0) in a fresh JVM (seeding it from the world seed broke 41 of 121 Bryce chunks). Fix `72600f9e`, pin make, -C, csrc, test, (test_chunks).
- **worldgen-surface-quirks** (2026-09-22, lane/worldgen (chunk dumps)): ported as read: the surface pass writes column (x,z) transposed against the density pass; the swamp lily loop never places; a mutated biome runs its base surface; biome height weighting is float (-ffp-contract=off). Fix `72600f9e`, pin make, -C, csrc, test, (test_chunks).

### light (4)

- **pf-endlight-g29** (2026-09-26, lane/playfuzz (playfuzz), field `w.ents`): a missing chunk section in the End or the Nether is dark to getBlockLightValue (g29:2901 row 791). Fix `ca360079`, pin out/java/snapshots/pf-endlight-g29.
- **l1parity-sky-pass** (2026-09-25, lane/l1parity): the sky-light pass ran in no-sky worlds (Nether, End) where Java gates on hasNoSky; no row differed (the no-sky store drops the writes), 98% of a Nether tick. Fix `e7fba285`, pin out/java/snapshots/nether-trip-s1.
- **populate-light-order** (2026-09-23, lane/populate (probe)): the light engine's add pass queued neighbours in x, y, z order instead of the Facing order its drain uses (one or two levels off over tens of cells everywhere); every older recording was re-recorded. Fix `83da427`, pin make, -C, csrc, test, (test_populate).
- **replay2-relight-checks** (2026-09-23, lane/replay2, field `d.sw`): load_world never copied each chunk's queuedLightChecks and populated flags, so native ran enqueueRelightChecks and its block light led the oracle's from tick 2 (47 chunks by 1500); first visible at row 1512 of the rain tape through the mood sound's nextInt(8). Fix `d70a8317`, pin out/java/snapshots/cg-replay2-still-rain-s1.

### physics (30)

- **slabfix-scratchlist** (2026-10-01, lane/slabfix (human run: the 496-world GRPO run)): a pool worker's thread scratch (one per worker, every env it steps) kept a collision list's grown slab block (a sweep past 512 boxes) for its next query: every later query on that worker, any env's, wrote its AABBs into the first env's image, which its reset had handed back to its slab, so a free block's chain link became a double and slab_alloc took a general protection fault (iteration 15 of bigA, twice); test_snapshots runs each env on its own scratch and never saw it. The block now goes back at the list's end (collide_scratch_end). Fix `7c2dd320`, pin scratch_check, (make, -C, csrc, scratch-check).

### client-state (60)

- **moon-swamp-s2** (2026-09-29, lane/playarena (gate)): play's live and --replay loops never enabled the grave (only --check and the judges did), so every dead or unloaded living kept its arena slot and a swamp's slimes at full moon filled all 65,536 at row 555 (play rc 134, the render gate's dumps; swamp-night-s2 the same); enabled in every mode, and the sweep skips a thread stack's MADV_GUARD_INSTALL guard page (glibc 2.43), which faulted once play's render threads were scanned. Fix `c7369119`, pin out/java/snapshots/moon-swamp-s2, out/java/snapshots/swamp-night-s2.
- **chainfuzz3-respawnvehicle** (2026-09-28, lane/chainfuzz3 (chainfuzz), field `d.cw`): the respawned EntityClientPlayerMP rides nothing; native's fresh client player kept the dead one's vehicle and snapped back onto it (pinned by pfc-deadrider-s6). Fix `9ec2a9e6`, pin out/java/snapshots/pfc-deadrider-s6.
- **covclient-fireballrest** (2026-09-28, lane/covclient (frame judge), field `px`): handleSpawnObject zeroes a fireball packet's data (func_149002_g(0)), so no setVelocity runs and the client copy starts at rest; native gave it the acceleration as its motion. Fix `82191634`, pin out/java/clientframes/cov-zoo-s1.
- **covclient-teleportlift** (2026-09-28, lane/covclient (frame judge), field `px`): handleEntityTeleport raises every entity 1/64; native's non-living branch had a 1.8-style tolerance and no lift. Fix `82191634`, pin out/java/clientframes/cov-zoo-s1.
- **covframes-slimesquish** (2026-09-28, lane/covframes (frame judge), field `px`): a slime or magma cube copy squishes by its own landings on the client (clientworld.c), not the server's. Fix `30d0e55a`, pin out/java/clientframes/cov-zoo-s1.
- **cp-end-s1** (2026-09-28, lane/pipeline (gate), field `d.cmath`): play's renderer hook (live_update_renderer) took the dials' surface test from view_dim, which follows a dimension change only after the tick, so at a respawn out of the End (and nether-fight-s1's death in the Nether) TextureClock and TextureCompass drew their Math.random off the surface where vanilla's theWorld is already the overworld: the client stream moved; play --check never ran the hook. Fix `697122e2`, pin out/java/snapshots/cp-end-s1.
- **cp-nether-s1** (2026-09-28, lane/pipeline (gate), field `d.cmath`): play's renderer hook spent the clock's and compass's Math.random only for the dials the loaded texture animation held; when lane/rerender retired rendertick/anim_liquids (play's fallback) none loaded and off the surface the client stream moved (cp-nether-s1 row 2, end-arrival-s1 row 38); the dials now draw whatever the animation holds, as vanilla and the engine's cp_renderer_draws do. Fix `63626ab8`, pin out/java/snapshots/cp-nether-s1.
- **pfc-brewbounds-f356** (2026-09-28, lane/chainfuzz3 (chainfuzz), field `d.blk`): the client's EntityItem.onUpdate push-out query (func_147461_a) reaches a brewing stand, whose addCollisionBoxesToList leaves the shared Block bounds at its 1/8 base, which the next pick and placement read; native's client item copies run no physics. Fix `bc7df800`, pin out/java/snapshots/pfc-brewbounds-f356.
- **pfc-hitfx-s7** (2026-09-28, lane/chainfuzz3 (chainfuzz), field `w.bc`): EffectRenderer.addBlockHitEffects places the particle in double arithmetic over (double)0.1F; native used the double 0.1, leaving the particle 1.5e-9 past the dug face so it slid past the end stone, came to rest a block higher and touched a brewing stand placed later, whose shared bounds changed early and moved the client's next pick. Fix `1004c764`, pin out/java/snapshots/pfc-hitfx-s7.
- **pfc-oreblast-s5** (2026-09-28, lane/chainfuzz2 (chainfuzz), field `d.cw`): the client's Explosion.doExplosionB draws BlockOre's, BlockRedstoneOre's and BlockMobSpawner's xp rolls on WorldClient.rand; the S27 now carries those blocks, and struct-tnt-s42 left clientdraw.allow (s5:1051 s4 row 381). Fix `a0fffa9d`, pin out/java/snapshots/pfc-oreblast-s5.

### oracle (35)

- **covclient-poolgl** (2026-09-28, lane/covclient (gate), field `px`): a warm pool member kept earlier jobs' GL state: RenderSlime's GL_NORMALIZE latch lit a later job's sign board darker than a fresh JVM (741 px, shading-offset); PoolGl restores the pristine capture between jobs (found by reasoning in lane/covframes). Fix `b75a8f70`, pin oracle/tests/pool-glproof.sh.
- **covframes-pickcap** (2026-09-28, lane/rerender, field `d.px`): not a regression: the goldens were drawn at partial tick 1.0 then 0.5 and the second draw moves the next frame (the pickup flight); coverage replayed the frames at 1.0 only and diverged at t=26 on every build since the recording. The oracle now draws --frame-pts' later ticks without writing PNGs and coverage.sh passes --frame-pts 1.0,0.5 for such tapes. Fix `b278f7a4`, pin out/java/clientframes/idx-pickcap-s1.
- **covnet-trsel** (2026-09-28, lane/covnet (coverage)): the agent's trade-select act built its MC-TrSel C17 with the byte[] ctor where GuiMerchant.actionPerformed uses (String, ByteBuf); Act.java builds it the vanilla way (the payload is the buffer's whole backing array). Fix `f0187b67`, pin out/java/snapshots/trade-s1, (trade-full-s1, trade-prof-s1).
- **rs-worldfx_underwater** (2026-09-28, lane/poolleak, field `d.px`): a warm pool member drew the first underwater frame with GL_FOG_DISTANCE_MODE_NV = GL_EYE_RADIAL_NV, left by any earlier job that drew a frame in air (EntityRenderer.setupFog sets it on the air path only, the water path never sets it, and glPopAttrib does not restore it); a fresh JVM has GL_EYE_PLANE_ABSOLUTE_NV, so the sky and every fogged draw came out 1 to 6 levels darker (t=25 bd7ad102f44526c2 against 9b4643d9e36fb900). PoolGl now restores it, and the pristine textures (lightmap, framebuffer, atlases). Fix `0e05cefc`, pin oracle/tests/pool-proof.sh, render.
- **autopool-detnbt** (2026-09-27, lane/autopool (pool proof)): a warm member's det.nbt listed EnchantmentHelper's and the enchantment table's split Randoms where a fresh JVM had not initialized those classes (the pool initializes every class at start); PoolAgent marks the split owners a run touches. - **cat-toss-ctrl-s1** (2026-09-27, lane/harness2 (audit OT-02)): Act.copyAgent did not copy ctrl, so an agent's "ctrl":1 drop threw one item instead of the stack. Fix `de00f754`, pin out/java/snapshots/cat-toss-ctrl-s1.
- **chainfuzz-poolrefusal** (2026-09-27, lane/chainfuzz): a warm pool member answered rc 0 for a next-start Snapshot that ran out of heap (Snapshot.parallel wrapped the OutOfMemoryError into an error reply), and was asked --save-at-end runs it always refuses; lane/poolfix makes any Throwable a fault that reruns fresh. - **dev-child-s1** (2026-09-27, lane/harness2 (audit OT-07)): dev summon's child flag did not reach PigZombie and the ageables (Dev.java sets IsBaby and Age -24000; dev.c ages before re-centring the half box). Fix `2445cc29`, pin out/java/snapshots/dev-child-s1.

- **inv-presses-s1** (2026-09-27, lane/agentscreens (audit RL-02), field `w.bc`): the oracle harness applied an agent's key bindings and hotbar under the inventory screen, which a person's keys cannot do (the screen drains them), so use presses placed blocks with the inventory open; Act now changes no binding under it (row 53 w.bc want 28 got 27 on master's harness). - **oracletick-stalesectors** (2026-09-27, lane/oracletick (merge with rerecaudit)): the first async chunk writer let a later save of a pending chunk replace the earlier, leaving timing-dependent stale bytes after each chunk's data in 1 to 22 region sectors; ChunkIO takes every save as its own copy in its own FIFO, written in save order. Fix `b95bfe2d`, pin make, -C, oracle, pool-proof.
- **rerecaudit-detailframes** (2026-09-27, lane/rerecaudit): --detail rows' x.cnew call sites carried the JVM's reflection frames (GeneratedConstructorAccessorN, numbered in inflation order), so two replays of mobspawn-final-s42 --detail differed. - **rerecaudit-lightrace** (2026-09-27, lane/rerecaudit): AnvilChunkLoader.saveChunk queued NBT sharing the live chunk's Blocks, Data and light arrays with the writer thread, so a relight before the write reached the file (combat-mobs-s1's save overlay differed run to run); ChunkIO.detach queues a deep copy. ## 3. Known and open (known unknowns, seen)

Every divergence between the Java oracle and the C engine seen and not fixed: the
ledger's open rows, then the sweep's
open and assigned clusters (master's out/sweep/causes.tsv), each with the
command or directory that shows it.

### worldgen (1)

- **pixworld-precip** (2026-09-27, lane/pixworld): the seed-30 mesa scene also differs in a precipitation height byte in chunk (1,12) (the dead bush at row 71 was the other half); not re-checked 2026-09-28 (lane/divgen): the repro was never kept and needs a new scene recorded (see repro). Repro: `lane/pixworld's out/java/try/diverge-found/pix-biome-s30 (not kept; re-record the seed-30 mesa script)`.

### blocks (4)

- **idxredstone-supportdrops** (2026-09-27, lane/idxredstone): blockcb.c sends the sign, trapdoor, ladder, carpet and end-portal-frame support drops through place_block_drop, which only the placement probe hears, so in a live world those blocks vanish without their item (the plates had the same bug); not re-checked since; re-checked 2026-09-28 (lane/divgen) by reading master aff2c569: blockcb.c still sends the trapdoor, ladder and carpet support drops through place_block_drop. Repro: `a scene that removes a sign's, trapdoor's, ladder's or carpet's support block`.
- **idxredstone-tickdedupe** (2026-09-27, lane/idxredstone): the ticks.c dedupe set keys on the exact block id while NextTickListEntry.equals uses Block.isEqualTo (a comparator 149 and 150 or a repeater 93 and 94 entry at one position); no survival path does that; not re-checkable 2026-09-28 (lane/divgen): no recording reaches it; stays open until a scene does. Repro: `no recording reaches it`.
- **playfuzz2-worldrand-g2** (2026-09-27, lane/playfuzz2 (playfuzz), field `d.sw`): the overworld's World.rand gains or loses a draw: grass random-tick counts match, so the static liquids, the tall-grass drop a torch placement set off, or setActivePlayerChunksAndCheckLight; row 374; re-checked 2026-09-28 (lane/divgen) on master aff2c569: run.sh g2:9004 FAILs row 374 d.sw as reported. Repro: `csrc/tests/playfuzz/run.sh g2:9004`.
- **projectiles-netherscene-gravel** (2026-09-27, lane/projectiles): a seed-42 Nether scene (MobFree radius 4, generateNether, no mobs) fails at row 123 on master code too: natively the overworld keeps falling gravel at (-181,13,-102) (67 extra entities, 7 blocks placed) that Java drops; not re-checked 2026-09-28 (lane/divgen): the repro was never kept and needs a new scene recorded (see repro). ### mob-ai (7)

## 4. Accepted differences (by design)

What the oracle or the native engine does differently on purpose, read live
from where each is documented.

### The oracle's pins (SPEC.md, "Pins")

Each pin fixes a vanilla nondeterminism (a race between threads, the wall
clock, a shared stream) the same way in the oracle and the native engine.

- **quiet-join pin** (`Oracle.quietJoin()`, oracle/harness/netherite/oracle/Oracle.java:35,63): while the client has not joined, `World.updateEntities` (oracle/src/world/World.java:1896, 1954) skips every non-player server entity and the weather effects.
- **calendar pin** (`Oracle.calendar()`, `calendarMillis()`): while the oracle runs, `World.theCalendar` starts at and `World.getCurrentDate` resets it to 2026-09-22 12:00 UTC, not the wall clock, so the date the bat spawn rule and the Halloween pumpkins read (and the snapshot's `calMonth`, `calDay`) is the same on every day a script is recorded.
- **save clock pin** (`Oracle.saveClockMillis()`): the wall clock vanilla writes into a save (level.dat's `LastPlayed`, so level.dat_old's; session.lock; each region chunk's timestamp) is the same instant while the oracle runs, and a chunk save queues a copy of its NBT (`ChunkIO.detach`: vanilla's shares the live light and block arrays with the writer thread) that the oracle's writer writes in save order, every save (a later save of a pending chunk does not replace the earlier, which would leave timing-dependent stale bytes in the region file's sectors), so two runs that save one world (a checkpoint, SAVEEND) write the same bytes.
- **tripwire pin** (`Oracle.tripwireBounds()`, one hook line in `BlockTripWire.func_150140_e`): the wire's entity test reads the block singleton's bounds, which in vanilla are whatever the last `setBlockBoundsBasedOnState` on any tripwire left (the client's pick ray, a mob's sight ray or an arrow on the server thread, a chunk render), a race between the two threads.
- **anvil pin** (`Oracle.anvilBounds()`, one hook line in `Block.getCollisionBoundingBoxFromPool`): `BlockAnvil` keeps Block's collision box, the singleton's bounds, which in vanilla are whatever the last `setBlockBoundsBasedOnState` on any anvil left (the client's pick ray, a chunk render, a ray on the server thread), so the server player's move against an anvil reads bounds the client thread set: a race between the two threads.
- **client consumer pin** (`Det.PIN_*`, no vanilla lines): vanilla's client thread draws one Math.random stream and one seeder for everything in call order, so the torch flicker, a spawned item's spin and bob phase (`EntityItem.hoverStart`) and every particle's flight depended on every other client draw of the session.
- **spawner display pin** (`Det.PIN_SPAWNER`, no vanilla lines; a tape's `start.spawnerPin`, on for new tapes): the mob a spawner shows (`MobSpawnerBaseLogic.func_98281_h`, made by the renderer on the first frame that draws the spawner) draws its `EntityLivingBase` constructor's three Math.random on the RENDER role's stream, whose state is every earlier frame-time draw's; the third is the head's turn.

- **clientrand-pin** (2026-09-27, lane/clientrand (frame judge), field `px`): vanilla's client thread draws one Math.random stream and one seeder for everything in call order, so the torch flicker, a spawned item's phase and every particle depended on every other client draw (the live client agreed with the tape's cmath on 0.1% of ticks); the client consumer pin (SPEC.md) gives each consumer its own stream reseeded per tick. Commit `23360d63`. Pin out/java/clientframes/cr-sweep-gold-g7-s42.
- **idx-spawnerhead-s1** (2026-09-27, lane/idxclient (index), field `px`): the spawner's display mob draws its constructor's three Math.random on the RENDER role's stream, so its head turn depended on every earlier frame's draws; the spawner display pin (SPEC.md) reseeds them per construction, and the client spawner is made by the S35 after its block. Commit `ec9a27c5`. Pin out/java/clientframes/idx-spawnerhead-s1.
- **mobscenes-deveffects** (2026-09-27, lane/mobscenes): the dev summon's "effects" do not apply attribute modifiers (ActiveEffects read from NBT), so a slowness effect does not pen a mob; a harness note for scene writers, both sides agree.
- **pf-anvilbox-g2** (2026-09-27, lane/playfuzz2 (playfuzz)): the anvil pin (SPEC Pins): vanilla's anvil collision box is the singleton's bounds that the last setBlockBoundsBasedOnState on any anvil left, a race between the client and server threads; the oracle uses the anvil's own metadata box, as native's collide.c does (g2-5005 row 178). Commit `33d34f32`. Pin out/java/snapshots/pf-anvilbox-g2.
- **rerecaudit-saveclock** (2026-09-27, lane/rerecaudit): the wall clock vanilla writes into a save (level.dat's LastPlayed, session.lock, region timestamps) made two saves of one world differ; the save clock pin (SPEC.md) fixes it to the calendar pin's instant. - **pf-wirebounds-g16** (2026-09-26, lane/playfuzz (playfuzz), field `w.bc`): the tripwire pin (SPEC Pins): vanilla's BlockTripWire entity test reads the singleton's bounds, which the last setBlockBoundsBasedOnState on any tripwire left, a race between the client and server threads; the oracle sets the wire's own metadata bounds first, the box native uses (g16:1606 row 44). Commit `ca360079`. Pin out/java/snapshots/pf-wirebounds-g16.

### Switched-off features (config.yaml)

A feature the native engine lacks is off in every world, in the oracle too
(WorldConf.UNSUPPORTED), so both play the same game; it leaves the list when
the native engine has it.

- `horses`: horses, donkeys and mules in the spawn tables
- `wolves`
- `ocelots`
- `built_golems`: iron and snow golems built from pumpkins
- `wither`: the wither, built from skulls and soul sand
- `minecarts`: mineshaft chest carts are never spawned (their loot is lost); carts cannot be placed
- `boats`: boats cannot be placed
- `redstone`: no block ever receives power: pistons, iron doors, rails and TNT stay put; hoppers only store
- `enchanting`: the enchanting table is a plain block
- `anvil`
- `brewing`
- `beacon`
- `maps`: an empty map stays empty; a filled map from loot never draws
- `fishing`
- `fireworks`
- `book_editing`
- `sign_editing`: signs are placed blank

Out of scope by design in the Java coverage's scope column (master's out/java/coverage/methods.tsv): 1083 switched-off methods (122 of them run), 826 client-cosmetic, 1651 not needed; 10034 methods in scope.

### Frames judged within a pixel budget

A budget line accepts a frame whose difference from the oracle's frame was
measured and named when it was accepted (a ratchet: a fix lowers it); every
judged frame has a line (frame_judge fails a frame without one). Per file (frame_budget: the
clientframes judges; render_budget: test_render's scenes; rawjudge_budget:
the Java-judged sessions) and recording family (the name's first word):
frames with a budget, those over 1,000 pixels off, the most pixels over 25 in
one frame, and the highest mean channel error.

### Raw input the judge does not compare (AGENTS.md)

Not judged: the cinematic camera (F8), keys the native client does not map (chat, the player list, F-keys), a Mac recording's keys beyond `--mac-keys` (Command as control), Escape in game (the oracle only releases the mouse, the native client pauses), window focus changes.

### What the playable client (csrc/play) lacks

Each line is a search of the native tree that finds nothing; it drops out when the client gains the part.

- No sound output: csrc/play/play.c opens no audio device (the sound calls' random draws are kept; nothing is played).
- No player list (Tab): play.c binds no key to it.
- The pause menu (Escape) draws only Back to Game: no Options, Achievements, Statistics, Open to LAN, or Save and Quit (closing the window ends the session).
- No title, options, video settings or controls screens: the player's keys, fov, gamma and mouse speed come from --options (an options.txt).
- No screenshot key (F2).

### Native functions no recording runs (make -C csrc coverage)

Not measured on this tree or master: no out/native/coverage/summary.txt (make -C csrc coverage).

### What the fuzzers never do

The generated sessions' blind spots: a line stays while its probe matches
none of csrc/tests/playfuzz/playgen.h, gen.c, ends.c, agentgen.c and
csrc/tests/rawfuzz/gen.c (csrc/tests/divergences_gaps.tsv). Directed
recordings may cover the same ground once; the random search never goes
there.

- typing in the chat or a command (T, /), and chat links
- creative mode, or any game mode change
- a difficulty change inside a session
- weather forced by a dev op (a storm on demand; rain comes only by itself)
- a name tag on a mob
- a mob spawner staged or sought out
- a dispenser or dropper
- a sign placed
- the options, video settings or controls screens
- F2 (screenshot) or F11 (fullscreen)
- a generated session as long as a Minecraft day (24,000 ticks): sessions plan 900 to 5,000 ticks; days pass only across chained sessions
- two players, or a second client: every start is single-player

The switched-off features (section 4) are attempted by the fuzzers (boats, minecarts, the fishing rod) but do nothing in either engine.

## 6. Unknown unknowns

They cannot be listed: a divergence nobody has seen has no row. What bounds
them is how hard the search has looked and how fast it still finds: the
number of new causes per 1,000 sessions, round by round. A rate that falls to
zero over many sessions says the fuzzers' action space is clean, not the game;
widening the action space (kits, new starts, section 5's gaps) has found new
causes at once every time.

### What would surface them

- The sweep running on every master (csrc/tests/sweep/sweep.sh): new seeds
  on every start, clusters handed to fix lanes, a fixed cluster that fails
  again reopened as a regression.
- Widening the action space: each gap in section 5 that a generator learns;
  new starts (other seeds, the Nether and the End, late-game saves).
- Coverage-driven scenes: each unrun in-scope method in section 5 run by a
  recording that checks it every tick.
- Human sessions (csrc/play/session.sh) and Java-judged speedruns
  (csrc/play/rawjudge.sh): the inputs no model of a player thinks of.
- Longer sessions: a Minecraft day or more in one session (random ticks,
  weather, the moon, despawns, village life at their natural rates).

## 7. C -> CUDA: device kernels and the pool against the C engine

Each device kernel (csrc/cuda) and the env pool's result (csrc/runtime)
are checked bit-exact against the C engine (make -C csrc gpu-lighting-check and
the phase checks). Its rows are the ledger's area `cuda`, open first.

### open (2)

- **cont-pickpart-s1** (2026-09-30, lane/invact (pool-gate AGENT=1), field `d.ents`): the pool's agent form diverges from the recording at row 87, the step of the script's {"press":["drop"],"ctrl":1} (oracle/tests/cont-pickpart-s1.jsonl line 56): the dropped item entity differs (want d.ents=f5c83977bad4ce91 got efd5d29467a27093); the same recording's tape rows pass the pool gate (452 rows equal), so the agent act's ctrl with a drop press (agent.c x->ctrl into act_agent) is resolved differently from the oracle's agent replay; not looked at further (the binding's nwrl_act carries no ctrl). Repro: `make -C csrc/runtime pool-gate AGENT=1 N=1 J=1 TICKS=1 RECS=cont-pickpart-s1`.
- **perfhost-cumesh-null** (2026-09-29, lane/perfhost (bench), field `frame`): the device mesher (cuda/mesh, render_blocks.c compiled for the device) reads address 0xc (a NULL pointer plus 12) inside render_block while explore-long-s1 envs walk into new terrain with groups of 4 or more envs a stream: every render after it fails (cumesh: counts: an illegal memory access; curender: the device mesher failed); never seen with groups of 1 (N 2, 400 steps); the host meshes the same sections. lane/meshcrash: the same class as renderprec-anvil-cumesh is likely (a draw leaves a warp split; the run kernels' overflow redraw fed unsynced shuffles: meshcrash-run-overflow-shfl, both fixed in a5fefea0), not proven: on the GPU host GPU 1, explore N 128 --env-seeds 50+300 steps, the fix and master 5cfcd77a both ran clean 10 of 10 and memcheck on the N 8 repro found 0 errors with the fix, so this set does not reproduce it. Repro: `pipe_bench --kinds explore --ns 128 --device-mesh 1 --env-seeds (full 100+1000 corpus, the GPU host; lane/popspeed saw 1 in 10 die); compute-sanitizer --tool memcheck out/runtime/pipe_bench --kinds explore --ns 8 -j 8 --device-mesh 1 --steps 150 --warm 2 (Invalid __global__ read of size 4 at $cumesh_run$render_block+0xaf1e0, address 0xc)`.

### fixed (11)

- **gputick-unmapped-write** (2026-10-01, lane/gputick (gate)): S4 on the device (fight-skeleton-s1 row 27, a falling block's adoption) grew a pool past the extents' mapped margin: the memset faulted the whole device context (Warp MMU fault), and the download's gather of the pages listed before it faulted again; the barrier now ends the run before an unbacked write and before listing its pages, and C redoes it. Fix `7161fa85`, pin out/java/snapshots/fight-skeleton-s1.
- **fall-s42** (2026-09-30, lane/s6lz (gate)): the S6 host build (cuda/tick/tick.mk, clang with nw-rename) compiled regioncache.c without REGIONCACHE_BUILD, so every S6 build keyed its region cache images by the build string "unknown" and read images an older S6 build stored; after lane/spillfast replaced zlib with lzpack the C trace of every recording whose seed world had such an image stopped at once with "a packed chunk (X,Z) does not decompress" (118 of 849 in the 2026-09-30 4:53 am batch gate: fall-s42, headline-s1, cov-villagerep-s42 and the others); the S6 host build now keys the cache by its own hash of the sources, the pass, its flags and its clang (out/native/cuda/tick/host/regioncache.build). Fix `008d91a1`, pin out/java/snapshots/fall-s42, make, -C, csrc, gpu-tick-check.
- **rlsplit-hudframe** (2026-09-30, lane/hudfix (gate), field `px`): the block outline (RASTER_CMD_LINE, 12 lines, drawn when the HUD is on, not under F1): the device drew it, as play's live frame does, but raster_obs_render (the C redraw from the frame data that pipe_gate compares against) had no case for it and skipped it; raster_obs_render now draws each recorded line through raster_overlay_line; pipe-gate CONFIG=configs/speedrun.yaml (hud on) MESH=2 DRAW=1: 129,056 rows, 32,288 frames bit-exact against the C redraw and the live C frames. Fix `e70c5006`, pin out/java/snapshots/sr-speedrun-s1.
- **dmeshflow-feed-twice** (2026-09-29, lane/dmeshflow (audit)): a mesh feed carried over frames nobody took (play draws between its dumped shots) could hold two uploads into one band or chunk slot, and cumesh_copy runs one block an upload in parallel, so the slot kept whichever block wrote last (never seen to fail: the later upload is usually the only one a request reads); a later upload of a slot in the same feed now turns the earlier ones into MF_SKIP. Fix `791f2145`, pin cuda-mesh-check, (play's, dumped, feeds).
- **meshcrash-run-overflow-shfl** (2026-09-29, lane/meshcrash (audit), field `count`): cumesh_run's overflow redraw (a lane's run past its 512-vertex buffer: every lane draws again) went straight into the full-warp shuffles of the vertex offsets with no barrier or warp sync (SASS: CALL then SHFL.UP, ptxas taking the warp as one), while a draw can leave the warp split (renderprec-anvil-cumesh): the offsets could be summed from lanes not there; not seen to fail on the dev host, a candidate for perfhost-cumesh-null (explore, many envs a stream: large launches take the run kernels). Fix `a5fefea0`, pin make, -s, -C, csrc/runtime, pipe-gate, MESH=2, DRAW=1, explore, stress, (pipe_bench, --kinds, --device-mesh, 1, --env-seeds).
- **meshsplit-fix-chunk-carry** (2026-09-29, lane/meshsplit (audit)): cumesh_fix held the lane after a redrawn one against its new end only inside a 32-lane chunk, so when a chunk's last lane was drawn again the next chunk's first lane kept the check made against the old end and could keep a start that was not its predecessor's end (over the MESH quick gate's four recordings about 100 such re-checks were skipped, the differing words piston_bounds, which the block gallery's pistons move and no later draw read: never seen to change a mesh); the check is now carried to the next chunk. Fix `40086eab`, pin cuda-mesh-check, (MESH, quick, gate).
- **meshtab-merge-redraw** (2026-09-29, lane/meshtab (gate), field `vertex 0 field 1`): merged with lane/meshgpu, whose write pass copies (mesh_wcopy) and redraws only requests marked MESHING_RES_REDRAW: mesh_tab_place left a request whose vertices did not fit its buffers at voff -1 without the mark, so its mesh came out zeros (1111 of 2153 on the quick gate, the counts and flags equal); it now sets the mark as mesh_run does, and world_raw the band origins meshgpu's band_of reads; never on master. Fix `fcd4bec2`, pin make, -s, -C, csrc, gpu-meshing-check, MESH_ARGS="--mesh-tab, pix-fluid-s1, cov-blkgallery-s1, cov-foliage-s1, cov-mesa-s1".
- **perfmesh-cocoa-cache** (2026-09-29, lane/perfmesh (gate)): rb3_render_cocoa kept its three icons in a function static, one for every device thread of the mesher module: a thread read the cache half filled and the device mesher took an illegal address (pipe_bench --kinds explore --device-mesh 1: the orchestrator's explore crash at N = 128, every run at N = 8 with the 32-lane kernel); the icons are looked up each time and mesh.mk fails a build whose device code keeps a writable global. Fix `ee2a18b0`, pin make, -C, csrc, cuda-mesh-stress, the, cuda-mesh, build's, writable-globals, check.
- **renderprec-anvil-cumesh** (2026-09-29, lane/renderprec (gate), field `frame`): the device mesher (cuda/mesh) fails on the dev host's RTX 3090 (sm_86) with an illegal memory access (cumesh: join, cumesh: draw) on master b1e35ce2 and on the merge base 25876549, even with groups of 1 env a stream; cuda-mesh-check pix-fluid-s1 meshes nothing (render_check --seq dies); the same gates passed on the GPU host (sm_120); maybe the cause of perfhost-cumesh-null. Cause (lane/meshcrash): ptxas spilled convergence barriers around the draw calls into the render tree; lanes returning first restored them while others still used them, so the draw kernels left the draw split and lane_end, compiled for a whole warp, read the mesher address from a uniform register another lane group had overwritten (memcheck: rec_state meshdev.c:311 from lane_end, 0x...00000040); only the ptxas -O1 build (PERF=0) crashed. Fix `a5fefea0`, pin make, -C, csrc, cuda-mesh-check, PERF=0, (93, recordings, pix-fluid-s1, among, them), explore, stress, at.
- **gpureview-L16** (2026-09-28, lane/gpureview (audit), field `light-check return value`): the device light engine (L16) lacked the client world's box rule (4ab41c3b) and three more gaps (chunk stamps set, not maxed; self-aliased reads at y 256 and -1; section creation order); fixed by lane/gpumirror, whose mirror rule now gates such changes. Fix `78b65e5f`, pin make, -C, csrc, light-check, (idx-cwlight-s1, in, the, quick, gate).
- **nether-trip2-s42** (2026-09-28, lane/pipeline (gate), field `px`): the device renderer keeps meshes by (section slot, version) and the C renderer counted versions per renderer: after the return from the Nether (tick 818) the new renderer's versions restarted and the device drew a resident overworld mesh for a new section (5404 px at tick 821); versions are now a process-wide serial (raster.c live_mesh). Fix `20340450`, pin out/java/snapshots/nether-trip2-s42.
